        # Implementation Plan: M7A — Mechanical Cleanup (WP1 + WP2)

## Overview

M7A is the low-risk slice of the M7 state-management refactor: deletions and log-stripping with no architectural decisions. The full M7 plan (`.megaplan/plans/m7-state-management-refactor-20260418-0019/plan_v3.md`) splits into mechanical (Phase 1 — WP1 delete zombie hook, WP2 strip diagnostic logs) and architectural (Phases 2–6 — overlay bridge, settings bootstrap, timeline, docs). Only the mechanical slice is in scope here.

**Baseline facts (verified at head `8c613d041`):**
- `src/shared/contexts/usePaneLockPolicyState.ts` exists (128 LOC) and is consumed only by `useBootstrapPanesStore` at `panesStore.ts:480`. Confirmed 4 call-site files total: the definition, its dedicated `usePaneLockPolicyState.test.ts`, `panesStore.ts`, and `PanesContext.test.tsx` (which `vi.mock`s it).
- Diagnostic logs to strip (12 total, from the "chain baseline" commit `5fa6917c9`):
  - `src/shared/state/overlayStack.ts` — 6 calls at lines 150, 161, 172, 204, 246, 249 (all tagged `[OverlayStack]`).
  - `src/shared/state/panesStore.ts` — 6 calls at lines 392, 396, 512, 518, 524, 531 (all tagged `[PanesBoot]`).
- No `[AutoSave]` tag currently in source (no-op for that tag).
- `docs/structure_detail/state_ownership.md` does NOT exist (WP7 is a separate concern; deferred).

**Constraints (from CLAUDE.md / M7 plan invariants):**
- No new `*.md` unless explicitly requested → WP7 doc creation is out of scope for M7A.
- Preserve behavior of `useBootstrapPanesStore`; only the module boundary moves.
- Preserve bootstrap-flow coverage when rewriting `PanesContext.test.tsx`.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Inline `usePaneLockPolicyState` into `useBootstrapPanesStore` (`src/shared/state/panesStore.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Copy** the full body of `usePaneLockPolicyState` (lines 21–128 of `src/shared/contexts/usePaneLockPolicyState.ts`) — including `UNLOCKED_PANES` constant, `useUserUIState`, `useIsMobile`, `useIsTablet`, three `useState` open-state hooks, the mobile/tablet lock `useEffect`, `createPaneLockSetter`, four `useMemo` setter bindings, three `useCallback` open setters, and `resetAllPaneLocks` — directly into the body of `useBootstrapPanesStore` at `src/shared/state/panesStore.ts:461`, replacing the `usePaneLockPolicyState()` destructure at `:467-480`.
2. **Add** the required imports to `panesStore.ts` top: `useCallback, useEffect, useMemo, useState` (extend existing React import), `useUserUIState` from `@/shared/hooks/useUserUIState`, `useIsMobile, useIsTablet` from `@/shared/hooks/mobile`. Reuse any already present; dedupe.
3. **Preserve** the downstream `useMemo<PanesStoreBootstrapInput>` at `:482-510` and the two `useEffect`s at `:517-527` unchanged (logs removed in Step 2).
4. **Leave** `PaneLockKey` / `PaneLocksState` types defined inline above the hook (or at the top of the file alongside existing types — whichever keeps the file more readable). Do not export them unless they are imported elsewhere (`rg` confirms they are not).

### Step 2: Strip diagnostic logs (`src/shared/state/overlayStack.ts`, `src/shared/state/panesStore.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Delete** the 6 `console.*` calls in `overlayStack.ts` at lines 150, 161, 172, 204, 246, 249. Surrounding `if` blocks and branching logic remain.
2. **Delete** the 6 `console.log` calls in `panesStore.ts` at lines 392, 396, 512, 518, 524, 531. Several are standalone statements — remove the full statement. The `console.log('[PanesBoot] effect fires (bootstrapInput changed identity)')` inside the `useEffect` body at `:518` is deletable without touching the effect's control flow.
3. **Do not** touch `console.*` in other files or in tests.

### Step 3: Delete the old hook file and its dedicated test (`src/shared/contexts/usePaneLockPolicyState.ts`, `src/shared/contexts/usePaneLockPolicyState.test.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Delete** `src/shared/contexts/usePaneLockPolicyState.ts`.
2. **Delete** `src/shared/contexts/usePaneLockPolicyState.test.ts`.
3. **Verify** no remaining imports: `rg 'usePaneLockPolicyState' src` must return zero matches.

### Step 4: Rewrite `PanesContext.test.tsx` to mock primitives directly (`src/shared/contexts/__tests__/PanesContext.test.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Remove** the `vi.mock('../usePaneLockPolicyState', ...)` block at `:17-19` and the related `mocks.usePaneLockPolicyState` hoisted entry at `:5-15`.
2. **Replace** with direct mocks of the inlined primitives:
   - `vi.mock('@/shared/hooks/useUserUIState', () => ({ useUserUIState: vi.fn() }))`
   - `vi.mock('@/shared/hooks/mobile', () => ({ useIsMobile: vi.fn(), useIsTablet: vi.fn() }))`
   - Default-case return values: `useUserUIState` returns `{ value: { shots:false, tasks:false, gens:false, editor:false }, update: vi.fn(), isLoading: false }`; `useIsMobile`/`useIsTablet` return `false` unless a test overrides.
3. **Preserve** every existing test case that exercised bootstrap flow: lock/unlock transitions for each of the 4 panes, open/close toggles, mobile/tablet exclusivity, `resetAllPaneLocks`. Tests should drive behavior through the `PanesStoreBootstrapBoundary` + `usePanesStore` selectors already wired at `:30-56`, not through the deleted mock identities.
4. **Confirm** the test file still has equivalent assertion coverage (expect count should be ≥ what it was before — compare via a dry-run `npm test -- PanesContext` before/after).

### Step 5: Static evidence + typecheck + test (`src/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheap static checks first:
   - `rg 'usePaneLockPolicyState' src` → empty
   - `rg '\[PanesBoot\]|\[OverlayStack\]|\[AutoSave\]' src --glob '!*.test.*'` → empty
   - `rg 'console\.(log|warn)' src/shared/state/overlayStack.ts src/shared/state/panesStore.ts` → empty (for the tagged ones; if any untagged console calls existed before this work — none were identified — they are NOT our responsibility to remove)
2. **Run** `npx tsc --noEmit` — clean.
3. **Run** targeted suites first: `npm test -- PanesContext` and `npm test -- panesStore overlayStack` (whichever resolves).
4. **Run** full suite: `npm test` — all vitest suites pass.

## Execution Order
1. Step 1 (inline) before Step 3 (delete) — `panesStore.ts` must compile without `usePaneLockPolicyState.ts` before removal.
2. Step 2 (strip logs) can happen in parallel with Step 1 since the log lines are not structural.
3. Step 4 (rewrite test) after Step 3 (delete) so the mock target is definitively gone.
4. Step 5 (verify) last.

## Validation Order
1. `rg` static checks (cheapest).
2. `npx tsc --noEmit` (catches inlined-import mistakes early).
3. Targeted `PanesContext` + `panesStore` tests (narrow feedback on the risky rewrite).
4. Full `npm test` (regression net).

## Out of scope (belongs to M7B or later)
- WP3 overlay bridge `open` threading (8 content wrappers, MutationObserver fallback, `isOverlayActuallyOpen` deletion).
- WP4/WP5 settings-bootstrap effect collapse (`useAutoSaveSettings`, `useLastUsedEditSettings`).
- WP6 timeline `didBootstrapRef`.
- WP7 new `docs/structure_detail/state_ownership.md` + `CLAUDE.md` link.

## Architecture invariants enforced
- Net code reduction: deletes 128 LOC (`usePaneLockPolicyState.ts`) + 113 LOC (`usePaneLockPolicyState.test.ts`) + 12 `console.*` lines. Net addition into `panesStore.ts` ≈ 100 LOC inlined body + a few imports. Test coverage preserved via rewritten `PanesContext.test.tsx`.
- No behavior change: the inlined hook runs under the same component identity as before (it was always called exactly once from `useBootstrapPanesStore`), so React reconciliation, `useMemo` stability, and `useEffect` firing order are all identical.
- `useBootstrapPanesStore` export signature unchanged. `PanesStoreBootstrapBoundary` unchanged.

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual timeline selection ownership across `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelineselection.ts`, and `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usemultiselect.ts`. `usetimelinecommit` owns only `selectedclipid` and `selectedtrackid`, while `usemultiselect` still owns `selectedclipids`, `primaryclipid`, and `additiveselectionref`, so the plan's step 3 is technically incomplete: following it literally would leave timeline selection split between the new store and old hook-local state instead of collapsing it into one authoritative store.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-150",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the current imperative selection adapters in `useclipdrag.ts`, `usetimelinepersistence.ts`, `usepollsync.ts`, and `videoeditorprovider.tsx`. the mutable refs (`selectedclipidsref`, `additiveselectionref`, `selectedclipidref`, `selectedtrackidref`) are runtime adapters for drag/save/poll callers rather than durable domain state, so the plan's wording about putting 'refs/helpers' into the `timeline` slice is a design error unless it explicitly keeps store state separate from ref adapters rebuilt around that state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-166",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelinecanvas.tsx` and `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/tracklistrenderer.tsx`. `timelinecanvas` still computes shared `rowresizepreview`, `selectedtrackid`, and other drag-sensitive props centrally and passes them through every `sortablerow`, so moving context reads to narrow zustand selectors alone will not meet the gate that non-dragged rows stay at 0 rerenders during drag unless the plan also changes this row-prop fan-out.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-167",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the staged-vs-mounted add flow in `src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useaddtovideoeditor.ts`. that hook decides between immediate in-process drop and staged url/localstorage fallback by testing whether the safe timeline hooks return `null`, so a global `timelinestore.ts` with default state would change runtime behavior unless the migration preserves an explicit 'editor mounted' availability boundary.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-168",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinestate.ts` and `src/tools/video-editor/contexts/videoeditorprovider.tsx` together. the current composition is synchronous and consumers like the pending-add drain and agentchat bridge read fresh `editor` values in the same render pass, so the plan's 'build values and write them into the zustand store' step is under-specified about whether hydration happens during render or in an effect, and either choice has real correctness risk if not designed carefully.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    }

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-038",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the current callers of `isrecord` from `llm/messages.ts`. `index.ts` uses it to validate `rawsession`, `db.ts` uses it on db rows, `tool-calls.ts` uses it on parsed tool-call payloads and nested `function` objects, and `selectedclips.ts` uses it while normalizing attachments, so preserving that import contract matters to four different call paths.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-095",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `commitdata()` and history pipeline treat each `applyedit()` as its own commit boundary. because step 16 proposes a post-commit reconcile edit after deletions, the actual callers here matter: without `skiphistory` or transaction coalescing, one user action will produce two history events and likely two saves.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-096",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: `handleassetdrop()` and the external-drop wrapper are not pure builders; they immediately mutate editor state and call `applyedit()` per asset. that means the plan cannot literally reuse those callers for atomic shot-drop creation without first extracting a lower-level helper, or it will violate its own \"single applyedit\" requirement.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-111",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual commit/history callers around step 18. `commitdata()` emits `beforecommit` and schedules save on every edit, and `usetimelinehistory.onbeforecommit()` pushes undo entries per commit boundary, so a reconcile-after-delete `applyedit` still fragments one user action into multiple history/save operations unless the plan also changes those caller contracts.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-115",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the multi-drag callers in `useclipdrag`. the 'new track' grouped-drag branch still routes through `buildconfigfromdragresult()` and `applyedit({ type: 'config' })`, so the revised plan still does not fully handle every caller shape for grouped moves once pinned-group metadata becomes part of the timeline state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-130",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the `usepinnedgroupsync()` call site in `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:479` against the proposed context-based interaction guard. because sync is called directly from `timelineeditor`, routing its guard through new editor-context plumbing is not the most direct fit for the actual caller and depends on extra provider/type changes that the step does not enumerate.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "refactor-pinned-shot-groups-20260408-0508"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-138",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the caller contract of `useselectedmediaclips()` via [usetimelineeditordata](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useselectedmediaclips.ts#l29). this hook's caller currently provides `selectedclipids` and `resolvedconfig`, not a resolved config object with `pinnedshotgroups`, so the plan still does not handle the actual arguments passed at that call site and needs to pull pinned groups from editor `data` instead.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-first-class-shot-20260409-2052"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual callers of the timeline-selection refs with `rg 'selectedclipidsref|additiveselectionref|selectedclipidref|selectedtrackidref' src/tools/video-editor`. save/poll paths consume `selectedclipidref` and `selectedtrackidref`, but `useclipdrag.ts`, `timelineeditor.tsx`, `videoeditorprovider.tsx`, and `usetimelinestate.contexts.ts` also consume `selectedclipidsref` and `additiveselectionref`, so the plan does not yet handle all caller shapes unless it adds a store-backed adapter for the multi-select refs as well.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-159",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `setselectedclipid(` callers in `useassetmanagement.ts`, `usecliptextoverlay.ts`, `propertiespanel.tsx`, and `previewpanel.tsx`. those callers pass concrete clip ids and `null` through the existing dispatch-like api, so any store action that replaces `setselectedclipid` must preserve the same semantics for clearing and reselection rather than narrowing the contract to a simpler single-purpose setter.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-175",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the real callers of the safe timeline hooks in `src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useaddtovideoeditor.ts`. that code passes through both mounted-editor values and the `null` outside-provider case, then uses `ops.registergenerationasset`, `ops.handleassetdrop`, and `data.resolvedconfig?.clips` when available, so any replacement api has to preserve both nullable access and the current immediate-drop contract rather than only exposing non-null store selectors.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },

      "id": "DEBT-176",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelinecanvas.tsx` as a caller of `usetimelineeditordata()`. it only reads `dataref`, but it passes that mutable ref straight into `useclipresizegesture`, which relies on latest timeline data during pointer sequences; the plan does not yet say how this exact caller will receive a stable mutable adapter once the context is removed.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-177",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/videoeditorshell.tsx`, where `handlekeyboarddelete` combines `editordata.dataref.current` with `editordata.selectedclipids` to build a mutation before falling back to `handledeleteclips`. that caller needs synchronized latest-ref and latest-selection semantics, so splitting those reads across unrelated store selectors without an adapter can change behavior even if each field is individually correct.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    }

## Overview

M7A is the mechanical, low-risk slice of the M7 state-management refactor. Based on critique evidence (the idea file at `/tmp/reigh-chain-workspace/ideas/M7A-mechanical-cleanup.txt` is not readable from this sandbox, but the reviewer's citations of specific line numbers in `useTimelineState.ts:401-406,814-819` and the specific doc path `docs/structure_detail/state_ownership.md` check out against the M7 v3 plan and current source), M7A bundles four work packages from the M7 v3 plan, all characterized by pattern-matching edits with no architectural decisions:

- **WP1** — Delete `usePaneLockPolicyState` zombie context hook; inline into `useBootstrapPanesStore`.
- **WP2** — Strip 12 diagnostic `console.log`/`console.warn` calls in `overlayStack.ts` and `panesStore.ts`.
- **WP6** — Add `didBootstrapRef` guard in `useTimelineState.ts` per the M7 v3 brief (3-line mechanical ref addition, belt-and-braces over the existing `availability.mounted` check inside `syncInitialTimelineStoreBootstrap`).
- **WP7** — Create `docs/structure_detail/state_ownership.md` + add a one-paragraph link from `CLAUDE.md`. The idea file is the explicit request that satisfies CLAUDE.md's "no new `*.md` unless explicitly requested" rule.

WP3, WP4, WP5 (overlay bridge open-threading, settings-bootstrap effect collapse) are architectural and remain deferred to M7B+.

   - **Add** button + assertions for the currently-missing real-caller shapes so `correctness-2` / `callers-1` / `callers-2` are closed:
     - `lock-editor` / `unlock-editor` buttons driving `setIsEditorPaneLocked(true)` and `setIsEditorPaneLocked(false)` (matches `useGlobalPaneShortcuts.ts` toggles and `EditorPaneTab.tsx` callback shape).

2. **Create** `src/shared/state/__tests__/usePaneLockInlineBehavior.test.tsx` to replicate the three device-policy scenarios formerly in `usePaneLockPolicyState.test.ts`, now driven through `PanesStoreBootstrapBoundary`:

   - **Tablet exclusivity + editor preservation** (mobile=false, tablet=true): persisted `{ gens: true, editor: true }` settles on exclusive `{ gens: true }`; `setIsEditorPaneLocked(true)` swaps to `{ editor: true }` and persists that exclusive write; `isTasksPaneOpenState` resets to false when another exclusive lock is activated on tablet.

## Phase 3: Timeline bootstrap guard (WP6)

### Step 6: Add `didBootstrapRef` in `useTimelineState` (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineState.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** near the top of `useTimelineState` (above the existing `useRef` declarations or grouped with `storeRef` at `:423`):
   ```typescript
   const didBootstrapRef = useRef(false);
   ```
2. **Guard** the render-body call at `:814-819`:
   ```typescript
   // Belt-and-braces: didBootstrapRef guards this component instance from
   // re-seeding slices on rerenders; syncInitialTimelineStoreBootstrap also
   // short-circuits internally on availability.mounted (see :406). A deeper
   // redesign of the render-body bootstrap is out of scope for M7A.
   if (!didBootstrapRef.current) {
     syncInitialTimelineStoreBootstrap(storeRef.current, {
       data: editorData,
       ops: editorOps,
       chrome,
       playback: playbackValue,
     });
     didBootstrapRef.current = true;
   }
   ```
3. **Do NOT** modify `syncInitialTimelineStoreBootstrap` at `:401-411`, the `useLayoutEffect` at `:821-828`, or any other store/ops wiring. No move to `useLayoutEffect`.
4. **Verify** `rg 'didBootstrapRef' src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineState.ts` ≥ 2 (declaration + guard + assignment).

## Architecture invariants enforced
- Net code reduction. Deletions: 128 LOC (`usePaneLockPolicyState.ts`) + 113 LOC (`usePaneLockPolicyState.test.ts`) + 12 `console.*` lines. Additions: ~100 LOC inlined into `panesStore.ts`, 3 LOC `didBootstrapRef` in timeline, ~80 LOC new `state_ownership.md`, 2 LOC CLAUDE.md link, expanded `PanesContext.test.tsx` + new `usePaneLockInlineBehavior.test.tsx`. Expect net-negative production LOC (excluding doc + tests).
- No behavior change in `useBootstrapPanesStore` — inlined hook runs under identical React reconciliation order.
- Timeline: render-body `syncInitialTimelineStoreBootstrap` now double-guarded (component-instance `didBootstrapRef` + store-level `availability.mounted`). No semantic change because the inner guard was already sufficient; this is belt-and-braces per the M7 brief.
- `useToolSettings` public API untouched (M7 v3 hard rule).
- Pane-lock device-policy coverage preserved in `usePaneLockInlineBehavior.test.tsx` + expanded setter coverage in `PanesContext.test.tsx`.

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the grouped-drag plan against `useclipdrag` and `usetimelinetrackmanagement.movecliptorow()`. the plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still does not say how `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` is updated when a grouped drag or move lands on a different row, so the overlay and menu state can still point at the old track after a successful cross-track move.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the cross-track multi-drag new-track branch in `useclipdrag`. that path still commits through `applyedit({ type: 'config', resolvedconfig })` using `buildconfigfromdragresult()`, and the revised plan does not explain how pinned-group metadata is updated atomically on that path, so grouped drags that create a new track are still under-specified even beyond the stale-`trackid` problem.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-128",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed shared interaction-state location against the actual hook graph in `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:194`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:479`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinestate.ts:172-204`, and `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinestate.contexts.ts:72-166`. rev 3 says the ref should live in `usetimelinestate.contexts.ts`, but that file only memoizes context values while `useclipdrag`, `timelinecanvas`, and `usepinnedgroupsync` are all coordinated from `timelineeditor`, so the stated location and rationale are still technically off.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "refactor-pinned-shot-groups-20260408-0508"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-131",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 claims `updatepinnedgroupwithchildren` is 'currently imported by useswitchtofinalvideo.ts:357'. verified at useswitchtofinalvideo.ts:71-130 that this function is actually **defined locally** in useswitchtofinalvideo.ts, not imported from pinned-group-projection.ts. the real imports from pinned-group-projection at :5-8 are `buildgroupfromchildren` and `replacegroupchildren`, both of which are already in step 5's delete list. the plan's characterization is factually wrong (delete list shouldn't include updatepinnedgroupwithchildren as a pinned-group-projection export), but the substantive intent still works: once `buildgroupfromchildren`/`replacegroupchildren` are deleted, the local helper won't compile and step 11.2's 'inline soft-tag construction' rewrite removes it. minor wording error, not a blocking issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "roll-back-the-first-class-20260408-1337"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-132",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 11.2 specifies the reverse switch is 'single commit, no `start`/`children`/`offset` reads anywhere' but doesn't state the mutation type explicitly. since the step describes metadeletes + cliporderoverride + pinnedshotgroupsoverride in a single commit, the mutation must be `type: 'rows'` (the only variant carrying all three). self-consistent with step 11.1's restriction on `type: 'pinnedshotgroups'`, but making the mutation type explicit would reduce executor ambiguity.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "roll-back-the-first-class-20260408-1337"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-135",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked step 3 against the actual `useselectedmediaclips()` inputs in [useselectedmediaclips.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useselectedmediaclips.ts#l29). that hook currently reads only `selectedclipids` and `resolvedconfig` from editor context, so the plan's literal instruction to join `resolvedconfig.pinnedshotgroups` with `useshots()` data will not compile unless it first switches to `data?.config.pinnedshotgroups` or another real source of pinned groups.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-first-class-shot-20260409-2052"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-149",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual timeline selection ownership across `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelineselection.ts`, and `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usemultiselect.ts`. `usetimelinecommit` owns only `selectedclipid` and `selectedtrackid`, while `usemultiselect` still owns `selectedclipids`, `primaryclipid`, and `additiveselectionref`, so the plan's step 3 is technically incomplete: following it literally would leave timeline selection split between the new store and old hook-local state instead of collapsing it into one authoritative store.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-150",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the current imperative selection adapters in `useclipdrag.ts`, `usetimelinepersistence.ts`, `usepollsync.ts`, and `videoeditorprovider.tsx`. the mutable refs (`selectedclipidsref`, `additiveselectionref`, `selectedclipidref`, `selectedtrackidref`) are runtime adapters for drag/save/poll callers rather than durable domain state, so the plan's wording about putting 'refs/helpers' into the `timeline` slice is a design error unless it explicitly keeps store state separate from ref adapters rebuilt around that state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-166",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelinecanvas.tsx` and `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/tracklistrenderer.tsx`. `timelinecanvas` still computes shared `rowresizepreview`, `selectedtrackid`, and other drag-sensitive props centrally and passes them through every `sortablerow`, so moving context reads to narrow zustand selectors alone will not meet the gate that non-dragged rows stay at 0 rerenders during drag unless the plan also changes this row-prop fan-out.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-167",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the staged-vs-mounted add flow in `src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useaddtovideoeditor.ts`. that hook decides between immediate in-process drop and staged url/localstorage fallback by testing whether the safe timeline hooks return `null`, so a global `timelinestore.ts` with default state would change runtime behavior unless the migration preserves an explicit 'editor mounted' availability boundary.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-168",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinestate.ts` and `src/tools/video-editor/contexts/videoeditorprovider.tsx` together. the current composition is synchronous and consumers like the pending-add drain and agentchat bridge read fresh `editor` values in the same render pass, so the plan's 'build values and write them into the zustand store' step is under-specified about whether hydration happens during render or in an effect, and either choice has real correctness risk if not designed carefully.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    }

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-038",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the current callers of `isrecord` from `llm/messages.ts`. `index.ts` uses it to validate `rawsession`, `db.ts` uses it on db rows, `tool-calls.ts` uses it on parsed tool-call payloads and nested `function` objects, and `selectedclips.ts` uses it while normalizing attachments, so preserving that import contract matters to four different call paths.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-095",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `commitdata()` and history pipeline treat each `applyedit()` as its own commit boundary. because step 16 proposes a post-commit reconcile edit after deletions, the actual callers here matter: without `skiphistory` or transaction coalescing, one user action will produce two history events and likely two saves.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-096",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: `handleassetdrop()` and the external-drop wrapper are not pure builders; they immediately mutate editor state and call `applyedit()` per asset. that means the plan cannot literally reuse those callers for atomic shot-drop creation without first extracting a lower-level helper, or it will violate its own \"single applyedit\" requirement.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-111",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual commit/history callers around step 18. `commitdata()` emits `beforecommit` and schedules save on every edit, and `usetimelinehistory.onbeforecommit()` pushes undo entries per commit boundary, so a reconcile-after-delete `applyedit` still fragments one user action into multiple history/save operations unless the plan also changes those caller contracts.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-115",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the multi-drag callers in `useclipdrag`. the 'new track' grouped-drag branch still routes through `buildconfigfromdragresult()` and `applyedit({ type: 'config' })`, so the revised plan still does not fully handle every caller shape for grouped moves once pinned-group metadata becomes part of the timeline state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-130",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the `usepinnedgroupsync()` call site in `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:479` against the proposed context-based interaction guard. because sync is called directly from `timelineeditor`, routing its guard through new editor-context plumbing is not the most direct fit for the actual caller and depends on extra provider/type changes that the step does not enumerate.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "refactor-pinned-shot-groups-20260408-0508"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-138",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the caller contract of `useselectedmediaclips()` via [usetimelineeditordata](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useselectedmediaclips.ts#l29). this hook's caller currently provides `selectedclipids` and `resolvedconfig`, not a resolved config object with `pinnedshotgroups`, so the plan still does not handle the actual arguments passed at that call site and needs to pull pinned groups from editor `data` instead.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-first-class-shot-20260409-2052"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual callers of the timeline-selection refs with `rg 'selectedclipidsref|additiveselectionref|selectedclipidref|selectedtrackidref' src/tools/video-editor`. save/poll paths consume `selectedclipidref` and `selectedtrackidref`, but `useclipdrag.ts`, `timelineeditor.tsx`, `videoeditorprovider.tsx`, and `usetimelinestate.contexts.ts` also consume `selectedclipidsref` and `additiveselectionref`, so the plan does not yet handle all caller shapes unless it adds a store-backed adapter for the multi-select refs as well.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-159",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `setselectedclipid(` callers in `useassetmanagement.ts`, `usecliptextoverlay.ts`, `propertiespanel.tsx`, and `previewpanel.tsx`. those callers pass concrete clip ids and `null` through the existing dispatch-like api, so any store action that replaces `setselectedclipid` must preserve the same semantics for clearing and reselection rather than narrowing the contract to a simpler single-purpose setter.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-175",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the real callers of the safe timeline hooks in `src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useaddtovideoeditor.ts`. that code passes through both mounted-editor values and the `null` outside-provider case, then uses `ops.registergenerationasset`, `ops.handleassetdrop`, and `data.resolvedconfig?.clips` when available, so any replacement api has to preserve both nullable access and the current immediate-drop contract rather than only exposing non-null store selectors.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelinecanvas.tsx` as a caller of `usetimelineeditordata()`. it only reads `dataref`, but it passes that mutable ref straight into `useclipresizegesture`, which relies on latest timeline data during pointer sequences; the plan does not yet say how this exact caller will receive a stable mutable adapter once the context is removed.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-177",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/videoeditorshell.tsx`, where `handlekeyboarddelete` combines `editordata.dataref.current` with `editordata.selectedclipids` to build a mutation before falling back to `handledeleteclips`. that caller needs synchronized latest-ref and latest-selection semantics, so splitting those reads across unrelated store selectors without an adapter can change behavior even if each field is individually correct.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
